- [ ] **A staging push landed mid-Lab-run and killed two paid judge experiments (2026-09-11, ~09:59 CH).** Commit `24a527c12` (scene composite off in production, another session) was pushed and deployed while the 5-reviewer bake-off matrix was running; hazard-count experiments 1160 and 1163 died with the container restart and had to be rerun (≈ $0.30 wasted, plus wall-clock). `.githooks/pre-push` is supposed to refuse while `GET /api/health/busy` reports a running Test Lab experiment — either that session bypassed the hook (`--no-verify` / `PUSH_WITH_OPEN_BUGS`) or the gate did not see the run. Needs: the pushing session's hook output, and a check that `busy` counts `scene_hazard_count` experiments (it reported `testlab: 1 experiment(s) running` for a `scene_composite` run earlier the same day). Same class as the 2026-08-05 kill in docs/decisions.md. **Evidence 2026-09-11 11:xx CH:** with experiments 1160 and 1163 still `status='running'` in `testlab_experiments` (orphaned by the restart), `GET /api/health/busy` returned `{"busy":false,"reasons":[]}` — so the probe does NOT count them (heartbeat-stale or stage-filtered). That is how the push passed the gate. Two fixes needed: the probe must report orphaned `running` rows (or a reaper must mark them `failed` on boot so the UI and probe agree), and the gate must treat a stale-but-running row as busy until it is reconciled. → `.githooks/pre-push`, `server/routes/health.js` busy probe, `scripts/admin/check-push-idle.js`
- [ ] **Two characters of a declared relative size are rendered as independent VB cells with no shared scale (found 2026-09-11, job_1789147573901_m3uam0nxi).** CHR001 is described as about as tall as a door and CHR002 as two thirds of that, but each character cell is rendered and gated on its own, so the stated ratio between them is never enforced — neither at render time (separate cells, no side-by-side) nor by any gate (both the character gate and the state-cells gate judge one entry at a time). Needs a decision: render size-related characters into one cell, or add a cross-entry scale question. → `server/lib/referenceSheets.js` `cellGatePrompt` / `checkStateCellsConsistency`, batch grouping at `groupElementsIntoBatches`
- [ ] **`syncVisualBibleSection` does not write a location's `vantages[].pages` back to the transcript** (found 2026-09-11 while wiring `applyBriefUsage`). It projects `pages` and `states[].pages` only, so a vantage's brief-derived pages live in memory and in `data.visualBible`, but a re-parse of `data.outline` (resume, the Lab) reads the bible-time vantage assignment. → `server/lib/beatsPipeline.js` `syncVisualBibleSection` (SYNCED_COLLECTIONS projection)
- [ ] **The face-prop one-entry-with-states rule has never run** (shipped 2026-09-11, prompt-only). The first beats story containing a map/letter/sign should be checked: does the bible emit one entry with a turned-away + face-to-camera state, does the AD cite the state id, and does the face state's cell carry the `text` words? → `prompts/story-bible-from-beats.txt`, `prompts/scene-expansion*.txt`, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-11 "A face prop is ONE bible entry"
- [ ] **`applyBriefUsage` does not run on the trial path.** `trialReferenceSheetPromise` fires inside `onVisualBible`, before any brief exists, so trial reference cells are still selected from the bible's own page guess. Decide whether trial should defer the sheet until its briefs exist or keep the speed. → `storyJobPipeline.js` (trial ref-sheet kickoff), `server/lib/vbElementBudget.js` `applyBriefUsage`
- [ ] **A reference sheet whose grid cannot be detected at all is still cut blind** (2026-09-11). `splitGridIntoReferences` falls back to the analyzer's `/split-reference-sheet` and then to equal-cell sharp math using the REQUESTED count; the merged-panel guard added today only protects the mismatch path, where a grid was detected. A detection failure on a sheet the model laid out differently still stores misaligned crops, silently. → `server/lib/referenceSheets.js` `splitGridIntoReferences` fallback branch
- [x] (2026-09-11, owner ruling) **A cell that fails its gate twice is still accepted and stored** (`vb_*_still_bad` — "accepted anyway"). CLOSED as intended behaviour: ship it. The gate stays fail-open on the second failure; it never drops the reference and never re-renders a third time. Consistent with the standing rule that a quality gate ships-with-warning on the final strike and never kills a paid run. The re-render is now told the reason (2026-09-11), so the second attempt is informed rather than blind — that was the part worth fixing. → `server/lib/referenceSheets.js` gate blocks in `generateReferenceSheet`
- [ ] **Action/interaction criticals survive repair, and we have not measured production's three passes against them** (2026-09-11, diagnosed not fixed). `job_1789147573901_m3uam0nxi` shipped p6 at 0 and p9 at 5 with 2 and 6 unrepaired `action_interaction` criticals. NOT a repair failure: `repairMaxPasses` is 1 on staging vs 3 on prod (`server/config/runtime.js:124`), and the single pass improved every page before the budget ended (p6 -20→0, p9 -15→5, p10 35→45). The type routes to `inpaint_or_regen` (`server/lib/evalBuckets.js:67`), i.e. re-roll the composition rather than a targeted fix. Open question: on production, do three re-rolls actually land an action fault? If not, action faults need a targeted repair. Needs evidence from prod stories, not from a staging run. Related: p10's critical ("no visible letters on the signpost for him to trace") was CAUSED by the VB trim bug fixed the same day — a page can be made physically unrepairable upstream. → `server/lib/repairPipeline.js` round loop, `server/lib/evalBuckets.js`
- [ ] **Some Visual Bible entries finish a run with NO cell-gate record at all** (found 2026-09-11 while fixing the state-cell identity gate, `job_1789147573901_m3uam0nxi`). ART003 (the central prop) and ART015 have `cellGates: []` — no `element_cell` and no `state_cells` verdict — so no gate ever judged their reference. Two candidate causes not yet separated: the entry had no reference cell rendered at all (the `appearsInPages` trim, fixed same day in `cdb334904`), or the gate errored and the fail-open path records nothing. The fail-open path recording nothing means a gate outage is indistinguishable from a pass in stored data; consider recording an explicit `{ok: null, error}` row. → `server/lib/referenceSheets.js` gate blocks in `generateReferenceSheet`, `server/lib/visualBible.js` `recordElementCellGate`
- [ ] **An entry with exactly ONE state is not a state batch and is gated as a plain element cell using the STATE row, not the parent description** (found 2026-09-11, `job_1789147573901_m3uam0nxi` ART015 "Felsblock", states `[blocking]`). `isStateBatch` requires `batch.length > 1`, so a single-state entry falls through to `checkElementCellRender`, which reads the cell element rather than the parent — worth confirming which description that question actually quotes before deciding if it needs the same parent-description treatment the multi-state path now gets. → `server/lib/referenceSheets.js` `isStateBatch` / `checkStateBatch`
- [ ] **The landmark-shortfall early abort now costs a scene-expansion call (2026-09-11, side effect of the AD authoring the Visual Bible).** `onVisualBible` fired at beats stage 3, before the briefs; it now fires after the all-pages Art Director call, so a retried attempt has already paid for the briefs. It still saves the scene review and the page text. Options: abort on the bible section as it streams, or accept the cost. → `server/lib/beatsPipeline.js` (bible-adoption block), `storyJobPipeline.js` `runBeatsWriterWithLandmarkGuideline`
- [ ] **The Test Lab `beats_scenes` stage now measures a bible it also authors.** `runBeatsScenesStage` no longer passes a stored `visualBible` (the AD writes one); the stage still scores only the page briefs, so a bad bible in a Lab run is invisible. Decide whether the stage should parse and report the emitted bible too. → `server/lib/testlab.js` `runBeatsScenesStage`, `server/lib/beatsPipeline.js` `extractBibleSections`
- [ ] **A total all-pages failure now loses the Visual Bible AND the cover hints, not just the briefs (2026-09-11).** Before, a failed AD call still had a stage-3 bible to fall back on; the per-page fallback now expands blind. Two batch attempts stand between the run and that state. Decide whether a bible-only retry (cheaper than a third full batch) is worth building. → `server/lib/beatsPipeline.js` (all-pages loop + bible-adoption block)
- [ ] **Idea-generation concreteness rule not yet seen on a real run** (shipped 2026-09-11, prompt-only, 4 local samples). Watch whether a concrete obstacle starts naming the test the characters must pass — observed once ("lets nobody through who has not first convinced him"), which the 2026-08-19 premise rule bans. → `prompts/generate-story-ideas.txt`, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-11 "The idea generator must name the want, the obstacle and the cost"
- [x] **GDPR erasure path built as an admin script (2026-09-11); all 8 questions ruled 2026-09-12 and applied (not committed).** `PrivacyPolicy.tsx:133` promises the right to erasure and `:151` claims self-serve deletion "through account settings" that does not exist; there was no compliant deletion path at all (`admin/users.js:642` deletes `orders`, which must be RETAINED, has no transaction, and misses 8 tables + 2 R2 prefixes). Shipped: `scripts/admin/delete-user-data.js` (dry-run default, `--confirm=<email>` interlock, one transaction, R2 after commit + verified, loud failure, `logs` receipt) and `docs/gdpr-erasure.md` (SOP). Ruled 2026-09-12: ledgers KEPT against a scrubbed sentinel user (Q1), third parties' rows untouched and the surviving code reported (Q2/Q3), abort on an unprocessed Stripe webhook confirmed (Q4), 10-year retention (Q5), policy now describes the email path (Q6), read-only `scripts/admin/audit-r2-orphans.js` for orphans (Q7), production-only R2 accepted (Q8). Two accepted limits split out as their own lines below; the changes are in the working tree, not committed. → tasks/gdpr-erasure-2026-09-11.md §8, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-12
- [ ] **Retained GDPR rows have no expiry and nothing will ever purge them** (accepted 2026-09-12, ruling Q5). The clock is 10 years (Swiss OR art. 958f) but no column records when an anonymised `orders` row or a sentinel-owned `credit_transactions` / `referral_payouts` row becomes deletable, and no second-stage purge job exists. Needs a retention stamp + a purge job before the first retained row ages out. → tasks/gdpr-erasure-2026-09-11.md §3, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-12
- [ ] **Self-serve account deletion button** (deferred 2026-09-12, ruling Q6). The privacy policy now points people at `privacy@magicalstory.ch` with a one-month clock, handled by the admin script; a user-facing delete button in account settings remains unbuilt. → tasks/gdpr-erasure-2026-09-11.md §8 Q6, docs/gdpr-erasure.md
- [ ] **The Visual Bible's `appearsInPages` is never reconciled against the scene briefs — 15 of 18 pages disagree** (measured 2026-09-11, `job_1789147573901_m3uam0nxi`; carved out of D21, which the owner closed on its gradient fix). ART001 declares pages [1,2,3,16]; p2's brief cites `["LOC002","ART011","ART012"]` and never mentions it, so a plot-critical prop is absent from the page that establishes it. Not a budget cut — 3 artifacts against a `VB_ELEMENT_BUDGET` of 3. p9, where that prop is unclipped (the plant for its p16 payoff), cites no objects at all; p7 drops both animals including the creature the page is about. `scene-expansion*.txt` rule 1 sources objects from the plan line only, and nothing checks the bible's own assignment afterwards. The semantic evaluator scored p2 100. Note rule 12e interacts: an unheld mounted prop can vanish even when cited, so listing it is necessary but not sufficient. → `prompts/scene-expansion-all.txt` (`objects[]` field rules), `server/lib/vbElementBudget.js` `rankPageElements`, `server/lib/beatsPipeline.js` `applyBriefUsage`

- [ ] **The arc telling rule ships unproven** (2026-09-11, owner declined the A/B at ~$1.04). `buildArcBudgetSection` now carries "One telling carries one thing the reader did not already know", with the matching critique question in both arc templates. - [ ] **VB element coverage, object light states, animals in EXPECTED CAST — owner-triaged 2026-09-12, fix approved** (measured on `job_1789163494908_kc2joi4ax`). Separately, `scene-expansion-all.txt:227` bans light from a state delta while requiring every page to fall in exactly one state, so the dragon egg's glow lives in `description` and the `dark` state claims the glowing pages 3/7/10 — the prompt then says "glowing egg" and "opaque dark brown" on the same page. And `collectSceneCharacterNames` never reads `objects[]`, where animals are filed, so the cat missed EXPECTED CAST and p12/p15/p16 took false `extra_character` CRITICALs (75/50/54). - [ ] **`landmarks/historical/` R2 objects have no delete path and the orphan audit cannot resolve them** (left open 2026-09-12 while fixing the other orphan causes). `keyForHistoricalLocationPhoto` builds `landmarks/historical/{rowId}-{slug}`; the slug is lossy, so no key-shape audit can resolve one, and no code anywhere deletes one when its row goes. - [x] **DONE 2026-09-13 (staging, not master) — A Lab experiment records the model it ASKED for, not the one that answered — the reasoning-OFF inventory arm is 2.5-flash wearing a 3.7-flash label** (measured 2026-09-13). Exp #1241 stores `params.model = 'gemini-3.7-flash'` with `reasoning:{enabled:false}`, yet 7 of its 10 unified outputs are byte-identical (MD5 + `outputTokens`) to exp #1237's 2.5-flash run, distinctive wrong answers included; `runVisualInventory` falls back to `gemini-2.5-flash` on an OpenRouter throw or non-200 and only warns to the log, which the experiment's `logLines` capture does not keep. The arm is void — reasoning-off on 3.7-flash is UNMEASURED and needs a re-run. Fix the record first: store the model that actually served each run (and the fallback reason) on the run object, so no future A/B can silently compare a model with itself. FIXED in `9af75ecc6`: `runVisualInventory` returns `servedByModel` (its own reassigned `modelId`) and the `inventory_ab` arm records it per run — confirmed in #1243/#1244. Reasoning-off on 3.7-flash stays UNMEASURABLE, not merely unmeasured: `{enabled:false}` returns 400 "Reasoning is mandatory for this endpoint", and `{effort:'low'}` (#1242) left output tokens at 2,781 vs the default 2,710 while failing 3 of 10 pages. → `server/lib/evalPipeline.js`, `server/lib/testlab.js`, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-13
- [x] **DONE 2026-09-13 (staging, not master) — `items_held` is the one field where the more accurate describer is the more dangerous one** (measured 2026-09-13, 10 benchmark pages). 3.7-flash beats 2.5-flash 0.4 vs 1.6 consequential errors/page overall, but both of its errors are FALSE held objects — ground-resting sandals, and a grounded plank called "held in both hands" while its own `objects[]` marks that plank `grounded:true` — whereas 2.5-flash's only `items_held` error is an omission. A false held object commissions a repair; an omission does not. The prompt does not say that an object which hangs, rests, leans or is strapped on and merely overlaps the hand is not held, and nothing cross-checks `items_held` against the same response's `grounded` flag. FIXED prompt-side in `62da2cdfa`: `items_held` is now a two-key `{left,right}` schema, each value an object name or `nothing`, contact verbs excluded — a free-text rule alone did NOT work (#1244: "held in both hands" merely became "gripping the edge of a vertical wooden board"). Controlled both ways in #1252-#1255: positives 2/2 on both models, no false positives across 12 negative figures. Two of the original ground-truth labels did not survive inspection — the plank and the straw ARE gripped. → `prompts/image-inventory-unified.txt:10`, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-13

## Spec-drift class — evaluator vs generator inputs (2026-09-14)

**The principle:** an evaluator must judge against the spec the GENERATOR actually received.
Nine instances were found and fixed on 2026-09-13/14, all the same shape — a contract stated in
one place, silently unmet in another, invisible because nothing asserted it. Each fix is one
shared resolver plus a behaviour test with a wiring guard so the divergent expression cannot be
rebuilt: `resolveEvalSceneHint` (3b3070dce), `resolveGeneratedOutfit` (e403345b1),
`resolveEvalImagePrompt` (e38b3095f), `resolveTextStagePictureSpec` (f5271d13d),
`resolveDeclaredAvatarOverrides` (dc0eebb93), `resolveCoverTextContract` (1f5101ef9),
`buildAuditPages` (2beda5425), `beatsReplayInputs` (8777a73ea),
`resolveSceneCastEntries` (ae05f18b6), `resolveEvalSceneDescription` (in 16a996a7d).

- [ ] **No `docs/decisions.md` entry exists for this class.** The rationale for all ten resolvers
      lives only in code comments and test names; CLAUDE.md requires an entry for exactly this.
      Highest-value remaining item — without it the next session re-derives the principle or
      "tidies" a resolver away → this file
- [ ] **Nothing validated end-to-end.** Thirteen commits changed what nine judges see. The suite
      pins the wiring; no story has been generated through the new inputs. Cover scores were
      predicted to move (a real contract + real VB nouns can lower a cover that scored well on a
      blind one, changing `pickBestVersionIndex` outcomes) → this file
- [ ] **repairPipeline protection list is missing VB secondaries.** A repair aimed at one figure
      can alter or remove a secondary the page legitimately commissioned; the presence derivation
      then reports `missing_character` against the repair's own damage. Now has teeth because the
      cover eval and presence derivation were wired to see secondaries. Confirm from stored
      repairs first, then prefer building the list from the page's resolved cast over extending an
      enumeration that must be remembered → `server/lib/repairPipeline.js`
- [ ] **Attribution: #35's work sits inside commit `16a996a7d`** ("delete the dead STORY_BRIEF
      argument") after two concurrent agents staged in the shared tree. Code is intact and tested;
      the rationale is not recorded under its own commit → this file

- [ ] **The semantic judge deducts for "rendered as a toddler instead of a school-age boy"** (measured 2026-09-14, Story A p14 → −10 on a faithful render; reproduced in Lab #1263: 6 semantic age findings on one page). This is the cast-drawn-too-young class the owner has ruled out for the generation side (age-band clamp is fine); the judge now emits it. Where does "school-age" come from — the text, the plan, or the judge's own reading? Prompt-side decision. → `prompts/image-semantic.txt`, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-14
- [x] (2026-09-14) **FIXED — Findings do not record which evaluator emitted them (#50)** — `sources[]` (the field repair routing already reads) is now stamped by EVERY emitter via the new `server/lib/findingSources.js`: quality, semantic, compliance, entity, the presence derivation (`final_checks`) and the book-audit reader pass. Carried across the two merges that dropped it — the consolidator's entity whitelists and `repairPipeline`'s `readerFindingsByPage` rebuild — into the stored shape; the Lab panel now reads the stamp instead of guessing "missing source = quality eval". No new field, no scoring.js change, routing pinned identical. 7 tests in `tests/unit/finding-provenance.test.ts`. → `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-14). Original finding: In Lab #1263 half the findings carry no `source` (`[?]`) and the other half say `three-stage`; a text-derived `missing_character` on Story A p8 ("Wir vier zusammen") could not be attributed to a judge. Stamp the evaluator name on every finding at the merge point. → `server/lib/evalPipeline.js` (findings merge), `feedbackConsolidator.js`

Both stored the SAME reason — `generation threw: [CHARACTER 2×4] pass-1 generation failed for <name>: The operation was aborted due to timeout` — i.e. the 120s `AbortSignal.timeout` on the Grok call, **not** a Gemini `IMAGE_OTHER` refusal and **not** a gate rejection (no Pass-2 structural-check failure is stored for either character). The throw escaped `generateComposited2x4`'s row loop, which had a second try provisioned but never used it. Random, not correlated with clothing/age/art style: each character succeeded in the other runs, all 'standard'/watercolor. **Downstream cost measured as zero on these runs** — the avatars-stage coverage top-up regenerated both sheets (score 9) before the images stage started (23:01:55 CH < 23:02:44 CH; 00:23:49 CH < 00:24:42 CH), so no page rendered against the seeded raw photo; the cost was ~13 min of latency and a duplicated paid sheet. ## Repair protection + cover/page parity (2026-09-14)

- [ ] **The repair protection list is missing VB secondaries.** `repairPipeline` keeps an
      enumerated list of things a repair must not disturb; Visual Bible secondary characters are
      not on it, so a repair aimed at one figure can alter or remove a secondary the page
      legitimately commissioned. Now has teeth: the presence derivation counts figures against the
      cast, so a repair that drops a secondary produces a `missing_character` finding against its
      own damage — and 37.7% of repairs regress (measured, n=3 stories). **Deferred by the owner
      2026-09-14 as too involved for one sitting.** The fix is NOT "add secondaries to the list" —
      it is to build the list from the page's RESOLVED CAST, so a new cast category can never be
      forgotten again. Confirm from stored repairs that a secondary has actually been damaged
      before building → `server/lib/repairPipeline.js`
- [ ] **Covers are not evaluated by the stage that catches prop and cast defects.** `threeStageResult`
      present on 0 of 96 cover records across 40 stories — the compliance evaluator does not run on
      covers at all. Cover prompts also carry no REQUIRED OBJECTS block (`promptBuilders.js:2109`),
      and `image-evaluation.txt:155` skips D-16b (held-object swap) without one, so it is disabled on
      every cover — the one surface where `holds:` is a declared field. This is the measured answer
      to the owner's "cover and normal pages must be the same" → task #51
- [ ] **Three cover eval endpoints still on the pre-`1f5101ef9` pattern** (`regeneration.js:4008-4023`,
      `:4357`, `:6523`); one appends "TEXT REQUIREMENT - CRITICAL" so a correct TEXTLESS cover is
      judged as missing its title. Plus `regeneration.js:4294` passes `-1/-2/-3` to `getActiveVersion`
      where cover keys are `frontCover`/`initialPage`/`backCover`, re-evaluating v0 instead of the
      active version; `tests/unit/cover-eval-inputs.test.ts:136-139` is named for asserting
      `visualBible` but never asserts it; the `holds` id regex exists in 5 copies with 2 disagreeing
      vocabularies → task #52
- [ ] **The compliance stage invented a holder declaration** — p7 of `job_1789348171785_9oxos7dwv`:
      "Kiaan is declared to carry Ofeli" appears in NO stored field (the brief gives the egg to Julian
      in two), the same version's summary names a third character, the consolidator turned it into
      "add red egg in Kiaan's hands", and the book shipped with two of its one magic egg. The
      consolidator half is fixed in `3546394d2`; the invention itself is untraced → task #50
- [ ] **Structured holder field on page briefs — NOT PROPOSED, evidence against.** 478 pages: ~0
      briefs give one prop to two characters (3 strict hits, all legitimate), 3 duplication findings
      on 1 page. `wornItems[]` is the precedent against it: 88% empty where present, and its `off`
      state has never once been emitted in 478 pages. Recorded so it is not re-proposed → this file

- [ ] **Six cited evidence stories are ALREADY GONE from both databases (found 2026-09-14).**
      `job_1788285785501_sbpfd0i8s`, `job_1788698812047_q5b1vuds7`, `job_1788724538469_n6ylqxq7w`,
      `job_1788727744192_gxf6gbywo`, `job_1788763045123_z8so79ngb`, `job_1788802404497_i1mm4yn6h`
      (+14 older cited ids). Findings resting on them cannot be re-verified. Decide per finding:
      re-derive from a surviving story, or mark the claim unfalsifiable. Prevention shipped —
      `stories.evidence_reason` (migration 038) → `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-14 "A story can be
      marked as EVIDENCE", `server/lib/evidenceStories.js`

- [x] **DONE 2026-09-14 — ages 6+ (no upper cap) now get the `journey` band; there is no
      `'standard'` shape band any more.** `resolveAgeBand` mapped everything from 6 upward to
      `'standard'`, for which `AGE_BAND_TEMPLATE_KEYS` has no entry, so `buildAgeModeSection`
      returned `''` — most of the product's readers got no plot-shape rules at all, which is how
      `job_1789420083330_5si0z6ze1` (age 8) shipped a father handing the child a key that removed
      the only obstacle, with no low point. Ages 6+ and an unknown age now resolve to `journey`;
      `age-band-journey.txt`'s age-specific framing scales via `{BAND_TITLE}` / `{READER_LINE}` /
      `{SHAPE_SCALE}` (MINI + "in small" at 5-6, full size from 7, "the reader" at 13+, an adult's
      book at 18+, no cap — a grandmother trying it out is a reader). The maturity tables were split
      onto a second axis, `resolvePacingBand`, so a 12-year-old keeps their own budgets and
      catalogue bands. → `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-14 "Ages 6 and up get the `journey` band",
      `server/lib/promptBuilders.js`, `prompts/age-band-journey.txt`
- [ ] **`prompts/story-arc-judge.txt:40` now has a dead clause** — *"Where the BRIEF carries no
      age-band section the whole rubric applies unchanged, and hard"* can no longer fire for anyone,
      since every brief carries a band section. Left in place deliberately (dead, not wrong);
      removing judge text is a prompt behaviour change that needs its own owner call →
      `prompts/story-arc-judge.txt:40`, `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-14 "Ages 6 and up get the
      `journey` band"
- [ ] **OPEN QUESTION for the owner: is the reader the OLDEST main or the YOUNGEST?**
      `resolveAgeBand` reads `pickMainCharacters().focus`, which sorts mains oldest-first, so a
      mother making a book for her five-year-old with both as mains gets the MOTHER'S band. Meanwhile
      `youngestMainAge()` (`server/lib/promptBuilders.js`) already encodes "the youngest main is the
      reader" and feeds the child critic. Ages alone may not separate "a grandmother trying it for
      herself" (she IS the reader) from "a mother making it for her child" (she is not). Raised
      2026-09-14 alongside the journey-band change; deliberately NOT changed.
      **Measured on staging (read-only, 123 stories with a cast):** 75 have ≥2 declared mains and
      ALL 75 are mixed-age. Under a youngest-main rule the SHAPE band would change for 20 (27%) and
      the PACING band for 69 (92%). The damage is child-child, not adult-child: only 1 story in 123
      pairs an adult with a child (Lukas 7 / Roger 45). Dominant patterns are Emma 5 / Noah 7 (47
      stories, pacing `standard` where the 5-year-old rates `journey`) and Levin 5 + three
      3-year-olds (17 stories, which get the 5-year-old's shape AND budgets).
      **Precedent already conceded:** `creatureToneLevel` was converted to `youngestMainAge` by the
      owner on 2026-09-13 after `job_1789227389389_z18dmvnt6` (mains Liz 5 / Ayan 8) briefed a
      `formidable` creature — "claws and teeth visible… it may loom" — into a 5-year-old's book.
      Same bug, fixed in one function instead of at the source. The peril filter is youngest-keyed
      too, so the inconsistency is already live.
      **Recommendation (agent, owner's call):** pacing axis → youngest main (it is a comprehension
      budget; fixes 69/75); shape axis → keep oldest main (the 2026-08-25 rule is about whose story
      it is, and the no-upper-cap ruling only makes sense if shape follows the oldest);
      `buildTopicWindowSection` stays oldest (genuinely about the protagonist). Do NOT add a "who is
      this book for" wizard field — it would serve 1 story in 123; `languageLevel` already exists as
      a first-class reader signal and `readerAge()` already prefers it over cast age. Caveat: this
      would split the two axes onto different age sources, and `pickMainCharacters`' docblock
      currently claims to be "the ONE place that decides who the focus character is — the story
      shape and the age mode must never disagree about it". Also note `pickMainCharacters` caps
      mains at 2 AFTER sorting descending, so it discards the youngest main outright on a 3+ main
      cast: a youngest rule cannot just reuse the picker.
      → `server/lib/promptBuilders.js` `pickMainCharacters` / `resolveAgeBand` /
      `resolvePacingBand` / `youngestMainAge` / `readerAge`,
      `tests/unit/creature-tone-youngest-main.test.ts` (the precedent)

## 2026-09-15 — from the staging verification-and-fix sweep (173 commits / 60 hours)

173 behaviour commits (~143 verified, 27 PARTIAL, 2 superseded, 0 broken). 33 fix commits landed on
`staging` and are **not pushed**. Per-page story review → `tasks/staging-story-review-2026-09-15.md`;
decisions → `docs/decisions.md` (ten entries dated 2026-09-15).

The structural half is the real problem: a generic element has no id to cite (`objects[]` takes Visual Bible ids only) and the creature's own page range excludes the page, so a silhouette can only be prose — which sits before the `**REQUIRED OBJECTS` / `**ART STYLE` split and can be compressed away. The options are an owner call (split a plate group when a page's own field states a whole-ground light change; make the discard at least loud; leave it) → `storyJobPipeline.js` (Phase 5a-pre-vantage, `repPageNum`), `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-17, "An element's size rides the pose line that meets it")

Promoting the type is a classification decision. Test Lab #1283 (a scene-review replay over this run) returns `[element_uncited] none` and `[prop_unheld] none`, so no reviewer-side rule reaches it either — 9d is written around "the id OTHER pages give that same element" and no page ever gives it one → `server/lib/sceneBriefCheck.js` `REVIEWABLE`, `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-17, "A bare citation lands where the bible already put the page")

No critic mirrors it. `applyBriefUsage` computes exactly this disagreement (`lost` / `emptied`) but runs AFTER the review, so the one stage that could fix a citation never sees it; on `job_1789584708605_rts4wqupm` it reported `ART003` emptied and nothing acted. Replaying the review against the ALREADY-rebuilt bible (#1283) does return `[vb_state_range] ART001 (pages 13,14,15 state mismatches)`, which is the damage — proof the critic can see it, and proof it cannot see it in time. A mechanical, language-free entry-level check (bible declares page N, page N's brief does not cite the entry) would have named both pages without any word matching — but it is a new brief-check type the reviewer acts on, which is the owner's call → `server/lib/sceneBriefCheck.js`, `server/lib/vbElementBudget.js` `applyBriefUsage`

But a rewrite length budget was removed with evidence one day earlier — a78e8fa7d, on the finding that the growth measured was the template's own audit keys, not prose. Attaching the image was declined in the parity pass on three grounds (the description exists for this purpose; the configured default scene-iteration model is not picked for vision, so a multimodal contract would hold only per-environment; free-form VLM judging has measured badly here). The way to settle it is an `iterate` stage A/B on a page whose failure is compositional — one arm text-only, one arm with the failed render attached — not another opinion → `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-17, "Iterate is the SECOND Art Director"), `server/lib/testlab.js` `runIterateStage`
- [ ] **Nothing critiques a REWRITTEN brief — owner sign-off wanted** (2026-09-17). `prompts/scene-review.txt` is registered as the critic of `scene-expansion-all.txt` (`scene-brief-generator-vs-critic`) and runs once, over the Art Director's briefs, before any render. An iterate rewrite replaces one of those briefs and is reviewed by nobody — so every rule the reviewer enforces (`element_uncited`, `prop_unheld`, `vb_state_range`, the cast checks) is enforced on the first brief and on no repaired one. Now that the rewriter is asked for the same structured fields, the mechanical half of `sceneBriefCheck` could run on a rewrite for free; whether the MODEL reviewer should too is a cost and scope decision. Adding the two iterate templates as `generators` of that registry set is the shape, and it makes every `scene-review.txt` change ask about the rewriter → `scripts/admin/sibling-registry.json` (`scene-brief-generator-vs-critic`), `server/lib/sceneBriefCheck.js`

It was also one of the two `REINSTATE_TYPES` on the iterate path, and it carried the ANIMAL half of the reinstatement backstop: `cast_unlisted` deliberately excludes animals (owner, 2026-08-16) and the dropped-id half of `checkDeclaredSet` only sees an id the PARENT brief cited, so a rewrite that brings a creature back in the prose and cites nothing is now caught by no mechanical check. Measured cost on the 11-round corpus: zero — its one hit, `rts4wqupm` p6, is still named by `object_dropped_from_declared_set [ART001]` — so the gap is structural, not observed. Two candidate shapes, both classification decisions for the owner: widen `prompts/scene-review.txt` 9d to the rewrite (see the "Nothing critiques a REWRITTEN brief" item above), or admit animals to the iterate-path cast roster. Pinned as a fact in `tests/unit/iterateBeat.test.ts` → `server/lib/iterateBeat.js` `REINSTATE_TYPES`, `server/lib/images.js` `castNamesForCheck`

The rule reaches the built prompt — `tests/unit/ad-iterate-parity.test.ts` proves it byte-identically at all four brief-authoring sites — and p15's rewrite opened its DEPICTS anchor with "the egg is matte and dark, no glow", which is the rule's own first example and is not in the parent brief (that says the egg "catches a faint orange glow"). One instance is not a verdict; what makes it worth watching is the position: `sceneIntent` sits at the very top of the image prompt, so a negation there is the most heavily weighted line in the frame → `server/lib/promptBuilders.js` `ABSENT_THING_RULE`, `prompts/scene-iteration.txt` 15b
- [ ] **A state delta's placement half is still cut by a 34-preposition / 32-verb vocabulary, one comma-segment at a time — owner sign-off wanted on replacing it with structure** (2026-09-18). Measured over every stored staging state delta (126 stories, 165 deltas): 110 untouched, 44 shortened, 11 emptied. The emptying is now inert (`resolveObjectState` keeps a delta the split would erase), and precision on the ten page-citations where it actually trimmed a prompt is 10/10 — but **12 of the 44 shortened lose appearance riding inside a segment the vocabulary judged correctly** ("cloth folded once and tucked into an open belt loop" loses *folded once*; "chain lies taut and horizontal above the waterline" loses *taut*; "in motion" is dropped as a lead preposition). Segment granularity is the defect, not the word list, so narrowing the vocabulary cannot fix it. The structural answer is `look` and `placement` as separate authored fields — costed and rejected for now in `docs/decisions.md` (four authoring sites, a legacy reader for 165 stored deltas, and no consumer that wants the placement); the measurement that would change the verdict is whether giving the forbidden clause a slot lowers the 33% rule-violation rate, which is identical today on the path that states the rule (`scene-expansion-all.txt`, 51 of 153) and the path that does not (`story-trial.txt`, 4 of 12) → `server/lib/visualBible.js` `splitStatePlacement`, `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "A split may SHORTEN a state delta, never replace it")
- [ ] **The reference CELL renders from the WHOLE delta, placement clause included** (2026-09-18). `referenceSheets.js:1063` builds each state cell's prompt as `description: "<base description>, <delta>"`, and `stateCellsGatePrompt` (:904) hands the same raw delta to the cell judge. `scene-expansion-all.txt` says a state's cell "draws the object alone" and that "a state defined by contact with another element … drags it into the cell" — yet 55 of the 165 stored deltas carry a placement clause, so a third of state cells are asked for a hand, a nest floor or a pair of claws in a picture that must show the object by itself. This is the one place the look/placement separation would buy something code cannot get today. Not touched by the 2026-09-18 fix, which is scoped to the page prompt → `server/lib/referenceSheets.js:904,1063`, `prompts/scene-expansion-all.txt` (the states rule)
- [ ] **A rewrite's `wornItems.location` came back holding a raw Visual Bible id** (2026-09-17, Test Lab #1292): `"wrapped around ART001.2"`. `sanitizeVbIdsInPrompt` substituted it before the image model saw it, so the built WORN ITEMS line reads "wrapped around dragon egg" and nothing shipped broken — but the stored brief keeps the id, and every other consumer of that field reads it raw. The Art Director templates say ids belong in `objects[]`; the iterate templates inherit no equivalent rule for the free-text fields → `server/lib/wornItems.js` `buildWornStateLines`, `prompts/scene-iteration.txt` `wornItems[]`

### 2026-09-18 — from the eleven-commit parallel fix batch (audit: "code doing the prompt's job")

Eleven behaviour commits landed on `staging` from eight parallel agents; the decisions entries are
all dated 2026-09-18 at the top of `docs/decisions.md`. Everything below is what those agents
found and deliberately did NOT fix.

- [ ] **The EVALUATOR's own `matches[]` names two figures the same on 123 staging / 71 prod versions BEFORE any rename** (measured 2026-09-18 over 1,194 staging + 317 prod stored versions carrying both `matches[]` and `bboxDetection.figures[]`). That is larger than the rename duplicate bug fixed the same day in `bc3cbe062`, and it is a different cause: this one is evaluator OUTPUT, not reconciliation. `identityAgreement` carries such a pair across a swap and adds nothing to it (pinned by a test), so the duplicate survives into every finding built on those names. Owner asked for it to be backlogged rather than fixed with the reconciler → `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "A rename target is taken by ANY match that holds it", *Deliberately not touched*), `server/lib/identityAgreement.js`
- [x] **CLOSED 2026-09-18 (`d1c9a9d1f`)** — **`checkIdentityAgreement` pairs greedily by nearest centre with no mutual exclusion, so it MANUFACTURES conflicts** (measured 2026-09-18, Lab set 66 / experiment 1324). Of 10 contested shipped pages examined on the pixels, **4 were not disagreements at all** — both witnesses named the cast correctly and the pairing invented the conflict; `job_1787867402809_z9bwoo3yp` p3 is the clearest (the evaluator named all five pirates correctly in order, but emitted a compressed ladder of boxes ~0.08 apart and all shifted right, so every name matched the figure one slot over). 36% of the 132 staging conflicts are decided by a margin under 0.05, 19% under 0.02, the closest shipped one at 0.002 — at those margins the pairing is noise, not evidence. Preferring the same-name pairing within a small margin (or a proper one-to-one assignment) needs no new data and no extra model call → `server/lib/identityAgreement.js` `checkIdentityAgreement`. **Done:** the pairing is now a global minimum-cost one-to-one assignment with a name-agreement bonus, and the comparison no longer puts a face centre against a body centre — versions with a conflict 76 → 32 staging / 20 → 15 prod, characters erased 2 → 0 and 1 → 0, and all four of the "nobody disagreed" pages now produce zero conflicts → `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "The evaluator↔detector identity pairing is a one-to-one assignment")
- [ ] **`repairPipeline.js:3814` MEASURES identity agreement and never reconciles.** The page record recomputes `checkIdentityAgreement` for evaluations that never went through `evaluateImageBatch` (a redetection after repair), stores the report, and stops there — no `reconcileIdentity`, so a conflict found on that path corrects nothing and the stored `matches[]` keeps whichever names the evaluator gave. Whether the repair path should reconcile at all, or deliberately only report, is undecided and unwritten → `server/lib/repairPipeline.js:3814`
- [ ] **The scene reviewer's checks 3b `[clothing_incomplete]` and 10c `[critique_in_brief]` name "CHARACTER DETAILS" as the outfit authority, and that block carries no clothing.** Verified on a real built prompt (`buildSceneReviewPrompt` over `job_1789681157795_wkt20ckod`, 70,373 chars): the reviewer's CHARACTER DETAILS is `buildStoryContextFields`' — age, gender, personality, strengths, flaws, special details. The Art Director's `{CHARACTER_DESCRIPTIONS}`, built from `clothingRequirements`, is the block that carries outfits. Either the builder should pass the per-character outfits or the two checks should be reworded to the briefs → `prompts/scene-review.txt:50`, `prompts/scene-review.txt:81`, `server/lib/promptBuilders.js` `buildStoryContextFields`
- [ ] **`server/lib/testlab.js:6998` hardcodes its own copy of the sendable clothing-fault list** — `new Set(['outfit_misattributed', 'removal_unstated'])` — instead of importing `REVIEWABLE` from `clothingCheck` (which does not export it). Harmless today: the dead string simply never matches now that the type is deleted. But the Lab's faults-in/faults-out count drifts from production's the next time the set changes, which is exactly what the `clothing-findings-producer-vs-consumers` sibling set added on 2026-09-18 exists to catch → `server/lib/testlab.js:6998`, `server/lib/clothingCheck.js:389`
- [ ] **`colourBefore` can read a garment's colour out of an adjacent eye/hair/skin phrase** — `"brown eyes, wearing boots"` makes the boots brown — when the garment itself carries no colour within the 4-word lookback. Reachable by construction in rule 3 `garment_colour_wrong`, but measured to fire on **0 of 120** stored findings (all reproduced with their source window captured), so it is a latent shape, not a live defect. It is the one mechanism that survived the `outfit_misattributed` deletion → `server/lib/clothingCheck.js:90`
- [ ] **`refEntry = { ...obj.entry, description }` overwrites only `description` while `englishEntityRef` reads `extractedDescription || description`**, so the worn-state strip is a silent no-op for any entry carrying an extracted description. It sits in the SURVIVING structured `state: "off"` branch, so the 2026-09-18 prose-matcher deletion narrowed its reach from 2,060 fires to 16 rather than making it moot. **Do not "fix" it without a decision:** of the 18 element-cases with a declared `off` row across 247 stored stories, none has an `extractedDescription` and the strip changes nothing, so repairing the shadow would make the structured branch start deleting text it does not delete today — it would CREATE damage that is currently zero → `server/lib/promptBuilders.js:4174`
- [ ] **`prompts/illustration-edit.txt:7` carries an undeclared `{REPAIR_TEXT_GUARD}` placeholder that nothing fills.** `editImageWithPrompt` supplies only `{EDIT_INSTRUCTION}` and `{ART_STYLE}`, and `fillTemplate` drops an undeclared key silently, so every edit prompt this function builds carries a bullet that reads as an empty line. Same class as the "spread value needs a placeholder" lesson. Worth finding out what that guard was supposed to say before deleting the bullet → `prompts/illustration-edit.txt:7`, `server/lib/images.js:5109`
- [ ] **The Grok moderation branch is entered on any message containing `400`.** The condition is `grokErr.message?.includes('content moderation') || grokErr.message?.includes('400')`, so a prompt-too-long 400, a bad-aspect 400, or any message that merely contains the digits `400` (a byte count, a request id) takes the "sanitize and retry" path and logs *"Grok blocked by content moderation"*. Narrowing it to the moderation code (`imagine:content-moderated`) is a behaviour change to WHEN a retry happens, so it was left alone → `server/lib/images.js:5109`
- [ ] **Delete the Grok moderation substitution table outright — prompt-vs-code, owner's call** (measured 2026-09-18). The correctness fix in `c2dc65d0e` makes the table safe; it does not make it useful. It has **never fired** in either environment (every stored Grok content-moderation block belongs to another call path — `styledAvatarGeneration`, the avatar attempt loop, `char_repair`, `scene_composite`, `generateImageOnly`'s own fallback), its precision on the 529 stored instructions this function actually receives is **0%** (4 would be rewritten, all 4 wrongly — "into the chest" inverted on a treasure chest, `buzz-cut` → `buzz-touch` ×2, "strike flat against the pillar" → "touch flat against"), and the verdict it tries to dodge is not about the prompt: Grok's error is `imagine:content-moderated` with a non-zero `cost_in_usd_ticks`, i.e. the image was GENERATED and then rejected. Options: **(A)** delete the table, a moderation block falls straight through to the Gemini edit — the only rung on this path that has ever recovered a refusal; **(B)** delete it and give this path the same rung the Gemini ladder now has (`rewriteBlockedScene` on the edit instruction, retry Grok, then Gemini), which makes the two sanitisers genuinely the same shape; **(C)** keep it as fixed today. The investigating agent read it as (B), with (A) as the cheap version → `server/lib/images.js:5129`, `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "A refused image prompt is retried as SENT")
- [ ] **`going into` → `pointing at` is shadowed and has never fired.** It runs after the body-part pass has already rewritten `into the chest` to `near the chest`, so it can only ever fire on `going into <non-body-part>`. Reordering it is a behaviour change to a rule option (A)/(B) above may delete outright → `server/lib/images.js:5129`
- [ ] **The two provider-refusal prompt sanitizers inside `images.js` are an unregistered sibling pair — proposed, NOT applied** (2026-09-18). `generateImageOnly`'s Gemini safety ladder and `editImageWithPrompt`'s Grok moderation table are the same judgement on the two halves of image generation (a provider refused → mutate the prompt locally → retry the same provider → else hand it to the other), and they have drifted in **both** directions: the no-op guard existed only on the Grok side until `d32aa9303`, and the 78-word list was measured and deleted on the Gemini side while the Grok table with the same false-positive failure mode was left standing. ### 2026-09-18 — from the two-commit identity/beats batch (docs merge `4ef21cc6c`, `97ccc4128`)

Two behaviour commits landed after the three-commit merge above; their decisions entries are the top
two of `docs/decisions.md`. Below is what those agents found and deliberately did NOT fix, plus the
one measurement the shipped change still owes.

- [x] **DONE 2026-09-18 (`a6cc517cf`, experiment 1325, ~$0.07) — measure the second witness's VOTE, which is the one thing the shipped mechanism does not prove.** **Answer: it does NOT merely echo.** 40 per-conflict votes over all 19 staging trigger versions — detector 32, evaluator 3, a third name 3, silent 2 — and it withheld a rename on **3 of 19 versions**, 16 of the 89 renames (18%). Against the pixel-judged set: **0 false vetoes** (all five detector-right versions renamed unchanged) and **1 true veto**, `rts4wqupm` p18 v0, the page the mechanism was built for. **1 missed veto**, `wkt20ckod` p12 v1 — the clothing-corrupted page `4ef21cc6c` predicted by name, confirmed on its own motivating page. Provenance is thinner than the "7 judged" this line claimed: exp 1324 ran UNPINNED targets, so only **4 versions were judged on their own pixels** and `m3uam0nxi` p2 v0 / `vxnu60yjg` p16 v1/v3 inherit a verdict from a different render. The three follow-ups the run produced are in the batch section below → `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "The third identity witness does contradict the detector"), `server/lib/testlab.js:1450` (`runIdentitySecondOpinionStage`). **Original ask:** `4ef21cc6c` proves the veto cannot make anything worse (1194/1194 byte-identical unless the witness contradicts the detector, 0 duplicates and 0 characters erased when it does); it proves nothing about whether the witness ever contradicts it, and it leans towards confirmation for reasons that are not evidence — the witness answers the SAME question, on the SAME badged image, under the SAME prompt (`server/lib/figureDetection.js:1222`, which ranks clothing first and demotes position to "a supporting hint only"), while all 19 stored answers came from `gemini-full`. The experiment: replay `secondOpinionIdentity` over the **19 stored staging trigger versions** using each one's stored page image, stored `figures[]` and stored `expectedCharacters` — ~19 Qwen-VL calls, **no DINO, no SAM, no story run**. **7 of the 19, across 4 pages, already carry pixel-judged ground truth** (`m3uam0nxi` p2 v0/v1 and `vxnu60yjg` p16 v0/v1/v3 detector-right; `wkt20ckod` p12 v1 and `rts4wqupm` p18 v0 evaluator-right), so it measures FALSE vetoes (a correct rename withdrawn) as well as true ones. It needs a small Lab stage — the existing `bbox` stage re-runs DINO + SAM + `gemini-full` and would not isolate the question → `server/lib/testlab.js:6950` (`STAGE_RUNNERS`), `client/src/services/testlabService.ts:502` (the `identity_second_opinion` row), `server/lib/figureDetection.js:1412` (`secondOpinionIdentity`), `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "A contested identity gets a third witness")
- [ ] **NARROWED by experiment 1325, and explicitly NOT pre-authorised — a differently FRAMED question for the second witness, on the clothing-corrupted class only.** The measurement killed the general version of this worry (the witness contradicts the detector on 8 of 40 votes) and sharpened what is left: on `wkt20ckod` p12 v1, where the corrupted channel IS clothing and the prompt leads with clothing, the witness went silent on one contested figure and backed the detector on the other — the one miss, exactly as predicted. The candidate shapes are unchanged: a reading anchored on position / the scene plan rather than on clothing, or a forced pairwise "which badge is X". Both change what the SoM identity call asks on the veto path only, so they also raise whether the second witness should share the first's template at all. **A prompt change and a classification change: propose the shape and ask BEFORE writing it** → `server/lib/figureDetection.js:1222` (the inline SoM prompt), `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "The third identity witness does contradict the detector", *Three caveats*)

It also uncovered and fixed a live defect: the file announced "only the eight points below" / "Answer only these eight" while TEN questions stood, so Q9 and Q10 were dead by instruction in the prompt that actually ran; both headers now say eleven and the test parses the prompt's own number-words. Still unproven against a live planner — the settling replay is in the batch section below → `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "A page's obstacle-holder is named in its plan line"). **Original finding, as written when it was blocked:** **IN FLIGHT 2026-09-18, written and NOT committed — blocked on file ownership.** The fix is a `plan-generator-vs-critic` pair (one question in `prompts/plan-check.txt`, the same rule stated to the planner in `prompts/story-beats.txt`), and that registry set is a **[block]** set over exactly those two files, so gate 9 refuses a commit that moves one without the other — and no honest `Siblings-Checked:` vouch exists, because the critic half is the half that does the work. Both lines are drafted; two code-only shapes were measured against 28 stories / 443 pages and both are dead (the aligned-arc-sentence signal fires on 263/443 pages for 1 true positive; the re-entry-gap signal on 124/443 for 1). Composes with, and does not depend on, the approved declare → review → apply re-plan redesign. **Original finding: a plan line can omit a figure the arc's own beat for that page names, and no check exists** (2026-09-18, staging `job_1789681157795_wkt20ckod` p17). Arc beat 16 reads "Tobias kicks earth back into the hole but cannot fill it as fast as three boys empty it"; the FIRST division's plan line for p17 is "Levin gripping a fallen branch, eyes on the earth below the stone, Max and Kiaan already digging beside him with bare hands" — no Tobias. The re-plan never touched that page, so the `castLostByReplan` guard shipped in `97ccc4128` cannot reach it: **p17 was never in the plan even in the first division.** `prompts/story-beats.txt:62` already states the rule ("Any character who forces or blocks something is in frame where it happens") and nothing measures it, so the page shipped with `FAULT[IMG][CRITICAL]: p17 — The picture omits Tobias, who the text describes as present, sitting on the stone, and actively interfering with the digging`. Whether a beat's figure is load-bearing on its page is a prose judgement, so the candidate site is a new question in `prompts/plan-check.txt` (the `plan-generator-vs-critic` sibling set), not a deterministic counter — a new check is a classification change and needs owner sign-off before it is written → `prompts/plan-check.txt`, `prompts/story-beats.txt:62`

The re-plan then re-divided 14 of 18 pages and deleted Tobias from two of them. A code-side phrase matcher is forbidden here (no prose pattern-matching in code), so the declared field that should answer it is the plan check's ROSTER — which currently cannot, because `prompts/plan-check.txt:19` says "Report every **name** the who column carries". Candidate fix: one sentence in that ROSTER section expanding a collective reference to the commissioned cast into the names it covers. That changes what every counter measures on every story, so it is the owner's call → `prompts/plan-check.txt:15` (§ THE ROSTER), `server/lib/planCounters.js:523` (`runPlanCounters`), `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "A plan line may carry the cast without naming it")

### 2026-09-18 — from the two-commit measurement/collective-cast batch (docs merge `a6cc517cf`, `d7c5da362`)

Two behaviour commits landed after the identity/beats merge above; their decisions entries are the
top two of `docs/decisions.md`. Below is what those two runs found and deliberately did NOT fix,
plus what the shipped changes still owe.

**From the witness measurement (`a6cc517cf`, experiment 1325)**

- [ ] **A confused witness can veto — the vote requires agreement, not coherence.** On `ll5dyf4k8g` **p4 v0** (a 4-conflict page) the second witness named a **third party on 2 of 4** figures, so it plainly had not read the page — and one coincidental match with the evaluator on a third figure **withheld all 9 renames**. The veto is defined as "evaluator + witness agree", with no test that the witness's answer is internally sensible, and on a page where it names people who are not in the story that agreement is a coincidence. Candidate shapes (none authorised): discount a witness whose answers include a name outside `expectedCharacters`, or require agreement on more than one contested figure before a permutation is withheld. Both are vote-logic changes and need the owner → `server/lib/identityAgreement.js` (`reconcileIdentityWithSecondWitness`), `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "The third identity witness does contradict the detector", caveat 3)
- [ ] **The witness silently falls through to `haiku-full` on a Qwen 429 — decide whether a substituted witness may vote.** 3 of the 19 targets in experiment 1325 met `Qwen-VL HTTP 429`, and `_somIdentifyFigures`'s fallback chain answered them with `haiku-full` without anything recording that the design's witness was not the one asked. One of the three (`z9bwoo3yp` p16 v2) produced a **withhold Qwen did not produce on the same page's other versions**, so the substitution is not cosmetic: the witness's identity is a function of rate-limit luck. Options: let the substitute vote (today's behaviour, undeclared), let it answer but not veto, or fail the witness call and rename as today — plus stamping which model actually answered on the stored conflict either way → `server/lib/figureDetection.js:1384` (`SECOND_OPINION_START_TIER`), `:1115` (`_somIdentifyFigures`, the chain), `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, caveat 2)
- [ ] **Two measured withholds have no pixel verdict and need a look before they count either way** — `job_1787777447720_ll5dyf4k8g` **p4 v0** (9 renames withheld; the confused-witness case above) and `job_1787867402809_z9bwoo3yp` **p16 v2** (4 withheld; the witness was `haiku-full` after a Qwen 429). They are the only two of the three measured withholds without a verdict, and the 10/12 agreement rate on the unjudged versions says only that the witness usually confirms — it cannot say whether either of these was right. Judging them needs eyes on the stored images, not a paid call → Lab experiment 1325, `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "The third identity witness does contradict the detector")
- [ ] **`identity_second_opinion` is committed but was never run on a deployed staging** — experiment 1325 was driven by a local process writing real Lab rows into the staging database, because nothing had been pushed. Verify the stage from the Lab UI once staging deploys, and decide whether the card wants a custom renderer: today it renders the target, the run log and the `note` line, and a reader cannot see the per-conflict votes without opening the row → `server/lib/testlab.js:1450` (`runIdentitySecondOpinionStage`), `client/src/services/testlabService.ts:502`

### 2026-09-18 — from the four-commit CHANGES-parse / pricing / Lab-parity / dead-ids batch (docs merge `14b39dc76`, `d19c2dc50`, `9533d919b`, `09934ae42`)

- [ ] **Two `cast_prose` format violations survive the fix, both the phrase `cast out Tobias dominant`** (experiment 1329, `job_1789681157795_wkt20ckod`). The parser handles them correctly — it cuts the subject at `dominant` and records the remainder — so nothing is mis-read and no refusal follows from them; they are the residue of a model appending a qualifier to a bare name. Left alone deliberately: the only remedies are a re-ask (rejected in the same commit, since 2 of 2 models broke the format on the first attempt and the parser now absorbs it free) or a prompt example, which is a prompt change nobody measured. Worth re-reading if the rate climbs above the 2-in-6-lines seen here → `server/lib/promptBuilders.js:6537` (`splitBareName`), `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "One line, one change")
- [ ] **An `action in` declaration is parsed, stored and REVIEWED BY NOTHING — the same gap the `action out` line above already records, now one verb wider.** `14b39dc76` split `new material` into `action_in` (no page-count obligation) and `material_to` (the merge half, balance-checked by number). The balance rule therefore no longer looks at the common case at all, by design — recognising an action in prose is language, not arithmetic. Same owner call as the `action out` item: whether a prompt-side review is wanted → `server/lib/planCounters.js:972` (`reviewPlanChanges`), `prompts/plan-check.txt`

**From the pricing audit (`d19c2dc50`)**

- [ ] **Run `scripts/admin/check-model-pricing.js --db=prod --warn-only` from the daily Railway housekeeping routine — proposed, NOT built.** It is the only thing that catches a vendor rate that moves after 2026-09-18; it costs nothing per run (catalogue fetches, no model call) and would surface the way the story-doctor routine does. **A pre-push gate is explicitly the wrong place** — it would block a push on a vendor's price change, which is not the pusher's fault. Needs an owner call because it is a recurring paid-adjacent chore → `scripts/admin/check-model-pricing.js`, `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "Every price in `MODEL_PRICING` is re-read from the vendor")
- [ ] **Grok 2.0's `+$0.01 per input image` charge is modelled nowhere**, so staging's render cost is understated wherever reference images are sent. `runtime('pageRenderModel')` is `grok-imagine-2` on staging and `grok-imagine` on production — a deliberate per-environment cost difference — and the $0.02 vs $0.04 framing is right at 1K low, but a 2.0 edit carrying one reference really costs $0.05. Every page render sends references → `server/config/models.js:1381` (`'grok-imagine-image-2.0': { perImage: 0.04 }`), `server/config/runtime.js:95` (`pageRenderModel`)
- [ ] **Two gaps in the `JOB_SPEND_CAP_USD = 15` hard throw.** `jobSpentUsd` prefers `usage.direct_cost` and falls back to the table, so (a) **a model with no table entry and no reported cost adds $0 to the cap** — before `d19c2dc50` that included `grok-4.6` on the non-`textModels` OpenRouter call sites — and (b) **Anthropic and Google never report a cost, so they are priced purely from the table**. The cap now counts more accurately, which makes it very slightly more likely to trip; measured headroom is fine (median full story $3.25 against a $15 cap). Neither is urgent; both are worth knowing before the cap is tuned → `storyJobPipeline.js:563` (`JOB_SPEND_CAP_USD`), `storyJobPipeline.js:606` (the estimate)
- [ ] **Four stored cost figures are now known stale or wrong, in files this merge did not own.** `docs/decisions.md:3442` says "the ~$4.4 production story" — the figure is a stale era average, not a table error (re-measured 2026-09-18: prod stories ≥12pp over 120 days run **$2.20–$7.02, median $3.25**, the two most recent $6.6 and $7.0); the same entry's note that "the repo's own price table has deepseek at $0.435/$0.87, which is 3.7x low and is filed in `tasks/BACKLOG.md`" should now say the table was corrected. `docs/decisions.md:17413` prices the beats reviewer at "~$0.08 vs ~$0.05 per story" — direction right, **gap ~4x understated**: from the providers' own billing, `beats_review` on `grok-4.6` is **$0.140/story** (26 staging) / $0.145 (11 prod) against **$0.029/story** on `deepseek-v4-pro` (16 staging), so the real gap is ~$0.11, not $0.03 (the verdict was quality-driven and is unaffected). `docs/decisions.md:36557`'s "~$4.66 story" is the same era-average class. `server/config/credits.js:63` says "keeps a typical story under ~$3". None is a behaviour defect; all four are numbers a human reads to decide → `docs/decisions.md:3442`, `docs/decisions.md:17413`, `docs/decisions.md:36557`, `server/config/credits.js:63`

- [x] **CLOSED 2026-09-18 (`09934ae42`, staging, not pushed)** — **`grok-4-fast` was a dead model id still wired as the Grok vision fallback at six sites**: `server/lib/evalJudges.js:30`, `server/lib/evalPipeline.js:175` / `:223` / `:2398`, `server/lib/bboxDetection.js:648` / `:717`, plus `server/lib/sceneValidator.js:958` and `textModels.js`'s Gemini-safety-block retry — and **a fallback fires exactly when the primary has already failed, so the failure is invisible until both are needed**. Same class: `grok-3`, `grok-3-mini`, `grok-4-1-fast-non-reasoning`, `gemini-2.0-flash`, `qwen/qwen-max`. **Done, and three of those six claims turned out to be wrong:** xAI does not 404 the retired ids, it silently **redirects them to `grok-4.3`** (controls 404 correctly, so it is a curated map), which means the calls *succeeded* while billing $1.25/$2.50 against a booked $0.20/$0.50 — a 6.25x/5x silent overrun plus wrong model attribution, with no error to notice; `gemini-2.0-flash` is **alive** (delisted, not shut down) and two false obituaries in comments were corrected; `qwen/qwen3.8-max` is alive despite being absent from OpenRouter's listing. One exported `GROK_VISION_FALLBACK = 'grok-4.3'` replaces the six copies (a no-op by construction — it is what the redirect already gives), `retired: 'YYYY-MM-DD'` + `redirectsTo` becomes a field so entries are kept and historical rows still price, and the guard extends the two artefacts the pricing audit shipped: `checkLiveness()` probes per model per vendor and reports **both** directions, and the unit test forbids any server file hardcoding a retired key (teeth verified by reintroducing the string). **Fires 0, failures 0, damage 0** — the fix is preventive, by positive test not by a missing counter → `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-18, "The Grok vision fallback names a model xAI still serves")
